Linebacker Grant Stuard suffered a hamstring injury during warmups before the game against the Chargers, and running back Dean Connors suffered a broken hand during the game and was scheduled to have surgery Monday, McVay said.

#2735. Yankees legend Jorge Posada reveals rift with team: 'I don't feel welcome when I go there'

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

One of the New York Yankees' franchise legends is at odds with the club. Jorge Posada, the team's stalwart catcher during the Joe Torre dynasty years, recently told the New York Post that he doesn't believe the Yankees want him to return to the club in any kind of role. Posada said:

"I mean, I could, but I don't think they want me there, to tell you the truth.

"I don't feel welcome when I go there, you know? Even in spring training, I don't feel like -- I'm just like, I don't know, I don't feel welcome. I don't feel like they want me there, so I don't go."

Posada, a four-time World Series champion with New York, did not elaborate on his comments or cite a specific incident that made him feel unwelcome in the organization.

Posada, now 56, first signed with the Yankees after they made him a 24th-round pick in 1990. He spent his entire professional career in pinstripes. Posada's MLB career with the Yankees lasted from 1995 through 2011. Over that span, he compiled 1,664 hits, 275 home runs, 936 walks, 1,065 RBI and a WAR of 42.7. Along the way, Posada earned five All-Star selections as the Yankees' primary catcher and twice finished in the top 10 of the American League MVP vote.

In 2015, the Yankees retired Posada's No. 20.

Since retiring as a player, Posada has held a number of baseball jobs, including a front office role with the Miami Marlins when his former Yankees teammate Derek Jeter was atop the club's front office.

As for his former team, the Yankees were recently eliminated from the postseason by the Tampa Bay Rays in the American League Division Series. The Yankees have not won the World Series since 2009, when Posada was still their starting catcher and still one of the team's leading contributors.

#2734. Demond Williams Jr. likely to be Washington's starting quarterback vs. Penn State

SEATTLE (AP) — Demond Williams Jr.'s time away from being Washington's starting quarterback did not last long.

Washington head coach Jedd Fisch said Monday that, barring any lingering injury issues, the junior out of Chandler, Arizona, would start the Huskies' home game against Penn State on Saturday.

The move follows Washington's 17-10 win at Nebraska this past weekend when Williams came off the bench in the fourth quarter and led the Huskies — who had managed just three points in the first three quarters — to an improbable comeback victory.

Williams finished 7 for 13 passing for 99 yards. No play was bigger than a 24-yard completion to Chris Lawson on a fourth-and-18 play with 1:42 remaining. That set up a 25-yard touchdown pass to Lawson on a catch-and-run two plays later.

"If Demond is feeling good, I don't see why he wouldn't play and start," Fisch said. "… He took some hits in that game, so we've got to make sure that he's good to go and that he feels right. If he feels right, hopefully we can play off the momentum of the fourth quarter."

Williams had been out since halftime of Washington's 41-24 loss to Iowa on Oct. 9, when he suffered a fractured rib on the Huskies' final drive of a first half. He had thrown for 69 yards and was sacked four times.

Transfer Elijah Brown replaced Williams and threw for 251 yards and three touchdowns, and then went 21 for 27 for 302 yards, three touchdowns and three interceptions in the Huskies' 38-26 win at Purdue on Oct. 16 while Williams continued to recover.

When he was injured, Williams had a 145.7 quarterback rating to begin the year, which ranked 10th in the Big Ten.

Fisch didn't name a starter coming out of Washington's bye week, saying only that both quarterbacks would be available.

Brown ended up getting his second straight start, but the UW offense struggled against Nebraska's defense. Brown went 19 for 30 for 136 yards in the first quarters.

Fisch put Williams in to provide a spark, and that's just what happened. Now, the Huskies will see if he can continue to do so.

#2731. Bayern Munich star Harry Kane ready for battle vs. Atlético Madrid

Harry Kane knows exactly what Bayern Munich is walking into when they take on Atlético Madrid on Tuesday night. The Spanish side has built its reputation on defensive organization, physicality, and an ability to punish opponents on the counterattack, and the Bayern striker is expecting a difficult challenge at the Metropolitano.

Kane, who will be facing Atlético Madrid for the first time in his career, spoke about the threat posed by Diego Simeone's side.

"I expect a tough game. It's my first time playing Atlético Madrid, we know what they bring, we know how dangerous they can be in attack on transitions. We know defensively they can be really solid, really compact, can be tough to break down. They'll be physical, they'll try and make it tough for us, they'll press us, especially in their stadium. So, we have to be ready for a really big battle tomorrow night," said Kane (as captured by @iMiaSanMia).

Simeone has also been complimentary of Kane, and the England captain was understandably pleased to receive recognition from one of football's most respected managers. Kane reflected on his journey, the challenges he faced early in his career, and the reaction to winning the Ballon d'Or.

"Thank you to Simeone. One of the best coaches there's been in our modern era, so yeah, to have this compliment from someone like him obviously gives me a lot of pride. I think since the Ballon d'Or there's been a great reaction, and maybe because my journey is a little bit different to some of the other Ballon d'Or winners and, the hard moments I had to go through, especially early in my career, maybe people didn't expect me to reach the levels that I have now, but through hard work and determination and resilience, I've been able to do that. Of course it'll be a great battle tomorrow night, and I'm sure he'll be doing his best to try and stop me and the team, but of course I appreciate those kind words from him," said Kane.

There is also some unfinished business for Kane at the Metropolitano. The stadium was the setting for Tottenham's defeat to Liverpool in the 2019 Champions League final, a painful memory that he would certainly like to replace with something considerably more positive.

"Right now it isn't the best memory in my life, that's for sure. But I'm looking forward to being back here. It's an incredible stadium and I've heard the fans tomorrow are going to be at a high, high level, so I can't wait to experience that as well. But yeah, this is the football journey. Sometimes you have good memories in stadiums, sometimes you don't. Hopefully we can have a good memory tomorrow. I play again here next week with England against Spain, and then hopefully at the end of the year we're back again for the Champions League final. So I have a chance, and we have a chance to raise that painful memory from 2019 for sure," Kane stated.

For Kane, Tuesday presents an opportunity to make a much better memory in a stadium that has previously brought heartbreak. For Bayern Munich, it is another major test of their European credentials against an opponent that will relish making life uncomfortable.

#2730. Biggest questions for World Series Game 5: How will the Rays get enough innings to avoid elimination (again)?

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The Tampa Bay Rays took their first step toward a baseball miracle with their 12th-inning comeback win over the Milwaukee Brewers in Tuesday's Game 4 of the World Series. That 4-3 victory means the Rays avoided the sweep and cut the Brewers' series lead to 3-1. That sets the stage for Wednesday's Game 5.

Tuesday's escape for the Rays was obviously an essential one, as it was an elimination game. The win also nudged the Rays' chances of winning the series from "all but impossible" to "merely a longshot." Across all of Major League Baseball postseason history, 42 teams have been down 3-0, and only one – the 2004 Boston Red Sox – has come back to win the series in question. That is, history suggests the Rays had a mere 2.4% chance of pulling off the miracle comeback when they were down 3-0. Now, though, they're down 3-1.

In all, 96 teams have been down 3-1 in a best-of-seven MLB postseason series, and 14 of them have clawed back to win the series. That particular data set says the Rays have a 14.6% chance of stunning the Brewers and winning this Fall Classic. Those aren't great odds, obviously, but they're much better than the odds the Rays were facing going into Game 4. Interestingly, teams down 3-1 have a .500 record in Game 5.

Speaking of which, that brings us to three questions for Game 5 – the answers to which will determine whether the Rays can cut the Brewers' advantage to 3-2 and send the series back to Milwaukee. Let's get to it.

Here's the big one. Rays manager Kevin Cash wisely managed Game 4 for what it was: a must-win contest. That means you worry about tomorrow when tomorrow comes and tactically do everything possible to survive the elimination game. Tomorrow, though, has now arrived, and Cash must figure out how to deploy his pitching staff for another elimination game after using seven arms on Tuesday. Game 2 starter Drew Rasmussen will be going on short rest in Game 5, but what then?

The most notable Game 4 decision was using Nick Martinez, who had been slated to start Game 5, in relief. Martinez worked two scoreless innings out of the bullpen and threw 33 pitches. It'll be another all-hands-on-deck affair on Wednesday, but just who those hands will be after Rasmussen is another matter. Griffin Jax started Game 4 for Tampa Bay and amassed 74 pitches, which presumably rules him out for Game 5.

As for the traditional relievers, Cole Sulser, Steven Matz, and Garrett Cleavinger did not pitch on Tuesday and thus can probably expect to be used in Game 5. Bryan Baker and Kevin Kelly did pitch in Game 4 but they didn't pitch in Monday's Game 3 blowout loss, which means they're presumably available. Ian Seymour also didn't pitch in Game 3, but his 32-pitch workload in Game 4 may rule him out. Tyler Wells and Cameron Booser have each pitched two days in a row, but desperate straits in October may mean they'll be called upon again. As for Rasmussen, he's made short-rest starts before, but he was basically an opener in those outings, the last of which was in 2022. This, then, is something close to virgin territory for him. No doubt, Cash won't lean on Rasmussen for a long start even if he's cruising, but they could use some bulk from him given the shallow and probably tired bullpen. This will be Rasmussen's fifth start of the 2026 postseason. Across his previous four starts, he put up a 2.19 ERA and 2.57 FIP with 27 strikeouts and five walks in 24 ⅔ innings.

In light of the maxed-out stakes involved, anyone who's even a remotely feasible option will probably tell Cash he's willing to pitch. As such, Cash's decisions on whom to trust and whom to assign the plurality of innings will be fascinating and perhaps determinative.

No such dilemmas are to be found for Pat Murphy and the Brewers, at least insofar as their Game 5 starter is concerned. Jacob Misiorowski, the 24-year-old NL Cy Young favorite and the hardest-throwing starting pitcher anyone's ever seen, on Wednesday will make his fourth start of these playoffs. Last time out, Misiorowski in Game 1 against the Rays went five innings and permitted only one run en route to picking up the win. "Miz" also enjoyed a velocity bump compared to his prior run of starts.

However, he was coming off 11 days of rest in that start, and for his Game 5 trip to the bump he'll be on the standard four days of rest. He's well into career-high-workload territory in 2026, so it remains to be seen whether the sizzle he showed in the series opener will be back for Game 5. The Brewers could use some length from him given how hard the bullpen worked in Game 4.

The Rays' best hitter and one of the richest power sources in all of baseball had to exit Game 4 in the 12th inning not long after reaching base on Joey Ortiz's throwing error. He did so because of an injury to his left foot. From afar, this seems like the kind of knock that would earn Caminero a few days off had it occurred in, say, June, but this is very much not June.

"Junior, he's fine and obviously in the lineup today. Really impressive that he went first and third right there, very instinctual play," Cash said on Wednesday.

"He's been dealing with some lower extremity injuries, but there's a lot of guys that are nicked up right now playing through a lot and know that Cammy will be ready to go today."

The heart of the matter is whether Caminero will be in top form less than 24 hours after sustaining the injury. That's worth pondering in light of his ongoing power outage. Caminero this postseason has a respectable .717 OPS, but he has only one home run in 48 at-bats. That's after hitting a whopping 42 homers during the regular season and averaging one every 14.7 at-bats. It's quite possible that the Rays will need some customary thump from the hard-hitting 23-year-old if they're going to extend the series. That we shall see.

#2728. What's next for Connor Bedard, Blackhawks after massive five-year, $75 million contract extension

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The Chicago Blackhawks have locked in their franchise cornerstone for the near future. On Saturday, the Blackhawks announced that star center Connor Bedard has signed a five-year contract worth $75 million.

Bedard's $15 million average annual value makes him the third-highest paid player in the NHL for the 2026-27 season. Only Minnesota Wild winger Kirill Kaprizov ($17 million) and the newly-signed Anaheim Ducks center Leo Carlsson ($18 million) are ahead of him.

"Connor has continuously defied our expectations since being drafted, and has quickly established himself as an elite player in the NHL," Blackhawks general manager Kyle Davidson said in a statement. "He utilizes all aspects of his game to not only be a constant threat, but to make the players around him better every time he steps on the ice. Connor's strong work ethic and determination to always improve his game has set an extraordinary standard for our young core, and we're excited for the incredible impact he'll have in this next chapter of Blackhawks hockey."

The No. 1 overall pick in the 2023 NHL Draft, Bedard has already displayed elite offensive upside, even if his overall game still has work to do. Last season, Bedard posted a career high 75 points in 69 games. In 219 NHL games, Bedard has totaled 75 goals and 128 assists.

The biggest issues for Bedard are durability concerns -- he's played fewer than 70 games in two of three seasons and will miss the first month of the upcoming season with a shoulder injury -- and subpar defense. The defense is a common problem for young centers and should improve in the coming years, but Bedard has now suffered multiple shoulder injuries in his career.

With Bedard's long-term contract finalized, both he and the Blackhawks will try to take the next step toward greatness. For the player, that means refining his game in order to become the $15 million man most believe him to be. For the team, that means surrounding Bedard with an adequate supporting cast for the first time in his career.

First and foremost, Bedard needs to get healthy and stay healthy. After missing 13 games with a shoulder injury last year, Bedard suffered another injury that required surgery to fix, and he will miss roughly a dozen games out of the gate in 2026-27. Those issues may delay his jump into the NHL's elite tier of players.

Once back at full strength, Bedard will have to show improvement defensively. He oozes offensive ability, but if Bedard is going to be a true No. 1 franchise center, he has to thrive at both ends of the ice. With Bedard on the ice at five-on-five last season, Chicago surrendered 3.60 xGA/60, per Natural Stat Trick. That was the second-worst number on the team with rookie Anton Frondell bringing up the rear.

That doesn't necessarily mean Bedard was the Blackhawks' worst defensive player, but it does mean he often struggled when going up against the opposing team's best players. Stars -- especially those raking in $15 million per season -- are expected to drive play for their team regardless of circumstance. Bedard didn't do that last season.

Bedard also needs to show more growth in the face-off circle. To his credit, Bedard has already done that, going from 38.9% as a rookie to 47.1% last season. Still, Chicago would probably like to see Bedard closer to 50%, especially with him taking key draws in big moments. Maybe that number keeps rising as Bedard gets strong and adds more tricks in the dot.

If Bedard can round out his game without the puck, $15 million per season will be a very fair deal, especially as salaries explode across the league.

We've already seen what can happen when a restricted free agent has all the leverage in contract negotiations. The Ducks were essentially backed into a $90 million contract after the Philadelphia Flyers signed Carlsson to a massive offer sheet.

That risk was there for Bedard too, which is why Davidson deserves some credit for signing him at a more reasonable cap hit. Bedard still has work to do in order to reach that price point, but his ceiling is worth at least $15 million per season.

The Blackhawks can help their young star by putting more NHL-caliber players around him. Chicago tore it down to the studs in hopes of landing Bedard in the 2023 draft, and while that plan worked, it also created a roster full of holes around the No. 1overall pick. Over the last three seasons, Bedard has had little help.

Chicago already improved its defense this offseason when it acquired Bowen Byram from the Buffalo Sabres. Say what you will about the acquisition cost or his $75 contract extension, but Byram inarguably makes Chicago better on the blue line. That unit still needs work, but it's a step in the right direction.

The real issue is finding Bedard a quality running mate at the top of the lineup. Outside of Bedard, only one other player topped 50 points in 2025-26, and it was 31-year-old Tyler Bertuzzi. Chicago still has $14.1 million in cap space with which to add some firepower, but some upgrades will have to come internally.

It's conceivable that the Blackhawks' lineup will include seven players selected inside the top 50 picks of the last five drafts.

That's an exciting proposition, and fans have reasons to be optimistic about the future. That said, none of those players have really established themselves as future stars, outside of Bedard. Frondell, Nazar and Rinzel seem like the best bets to get there soon, but that's based more on projection than anything tangible.

The first month of the season should be an excellent barometer for the Blackhawks as they assess those players. Bedard will be out of the lineup, and it'll be interesting to see who sinks and who swims until he returns. Once Chicago establishes exactly what it has in its young nucleus, Davidson can then go out and aggressively address needs via trades and free agency.

#2725. Karl's agent Ballack hits back at Bayern chiefs with strong defence of teenager

Amid a rather difficult spell since returning from injury, Lennart Karl has recently faced criticism from Bayern chiefs Max Eberl and Uli Hoeneß over his performances and behaviour off the pitch, with the latter suggesting that the 18-year-old needs to show more humility.

Speaking ahead of Saturday night's clash between Bayern Munich and Borussia Dortmund, former Germany international Michael Ballack came to the youngster's defence in his role as a DAZN pundit.

Ballack, who also serves as Karl's agent, made it clear that he considers the criticism of his client too harsh, pointing to his young age.

“Lennart is 18 years old. Where were you when you were 18? Just as he is finding his feet on the pitch, he is also trying things out off it. Social media is a major factor today, not just for the younger generation. Everyone is entitled to present themselves however they want," Ballack said.

“Of course, that does not mean getting everything right. Criticism is unfortunately inevitable. But you should not mix that up with his development as a footballer. And that is exactly what Bayern Munich are doing.”

Following Bayern's 6-1 win over 1. FC Magdeburg, Eberl said that Karl “needs to show on the pitch that he can get himself out of this slump” – comments Ballack found difficult to understand.

“For Max Eberl to now claim that Lennart is going through a dip in form, accompanied by references to missteps, just as his performances are clearly improving again, is difficult to understand in terms of timing and certainly questionable in terms of content,” he said.

According to Ballack, the fact that Karl managed to break through at Bayern at just 17 shows that he possesses not only considerable talent but also strong character.

“Lennart's development as a footballer has been outstanding over the past year. To establish himself at Bayern Munich at the age of 17 speaks not only to his exceptional footballing ability but, above all – and that is far more important at a top club like this – to his character. Lennart Karl has character. It is to be expected that this will rub some people up the wrong way once or twice during his career. I hope Lennart doesn't allow himself to be completely changed before he even turns 20. He has my full support," he said.

Karl eventually came on in the 84th minute of Saturday's 2-2 draw with Borussia Dortmund, replacing Michael Olise after the Frenchman picked up an injury.

#2724. Jets star goalie Connor Hellebuyck requests trade: Where three-time Vezina Trophy winner could land

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

After months of speculation about his future in Winnipeg, Jets goaltender Connor Hellebuyck has confirmed his trade request publicly.

Hellebuyck's name has come up in trade rumors since the 2025-26 season ended, and the rumor mill will only pick back up now that he's gone public with his request. NHL training camps will open soon, and Hellebuyck wanted to provide more clarity on the situation.

"Winnipeg has been home for my entire NHL career, and I will always be grateful for my time there.

"I have never forgotten June 23, 2012. That's the day Winnipeg took a chance on a goalie playing in Odessa, Texas. I gave everything I had from that moment to prove the Jets made the right decision.

"Several months ago, I privately requested a trade. I have tried to handle the situation quietly and respectfully while giving the organization time to explore its options.

"With training camp approaching, I felt it was important to publicly confirm my request. My position has not changed, and I believe moving on is the best path forward for me and my family.

"I appreciate everything Winnipeg Jets fans have meant to me throughout my career and hope a resolution can be reached soon."

In what would become one of the biggest steals in NHL Draft history, the Jets used the No. 130 overall pick to select Hellebucyk in 2012. Just seven years later, Hellebuyck established himself as one of the best goalies in the lea, and he's maintained that level of play for nearly a decade.

A three-time Vezina Trophy winner, Hellebuyck has a legitimate claim as the best goaltender in the world. Over the last three seasons, Hellebucyk has totaled 73.4 goals saved above average, per Natural Stat Trick. That's 20 more than the next closest goaltender -- Andrei Vasilevskiy -- and includes a rather underwhelming 2025-26 campaign.

There have been plenty of years in which Hellebuyck carried mediocre Jets teams, but 2025-26 was not one of them. He actually allowed 0.35 goals above average with a pedestrian 0.895 save percentage. Normally, it would be fair to worry about a 33-year-old goalie with those numbers, but not in Hellebucyk's case.

Hellebuyck backstopped Team USA to its first men's ice hockey gold medal in 46 years at the 2026 Olympics while allowing a total of six goals and boasting a .956 save percentage. He was exceptional in the final against Canada, where he turned away 41 of the 42 shots he faced and made one of the most iconic saves in hockey history.

THAT WAS ONE HELLE-BUYCK OF A STOP. 😳 pic.twitter.com/N3wCimdBGw

The biggest obstacle in a trade might be Hellebucyk's contract. He has five years remaining on his current deal at $8.5 million per season -- and while that may be a lot of term for a goalie his age -- Hellebucyk is worth a lot more than that at his best. The real issue is that he owns a full no-movement clause, which means Hellebuyck controls his fate, and the trade request going public certainly won't improve the team's leverage.

So, where does Hellebucyk land? Without knowing his preferred destinations, it's probably safe to assume that he wants the opportunity to win a Stanley Cup, and the team that acquires him will need the requisite cap space and resources to do so.

With those parameters in mind, here are five teams that could make sense as Hellebuyck trade suitors.

The Sabres were one of two teams frequently connected to Hellebuyck earlier in the offseason, and that makes sense. Buffalo is an ascending team with room to upgrade in the crease. Not to denigrate Alex Lyon and Ukko-Pekka Luukkonen, who were a formidable tandem in 2025-26, but I'm not sure Buffalo can bet on similar performances this upcoming season. Adding Hellebuyck would eliminate that question mark immediately and make the Sabres a favorite to win the Atlantic Division.

In fact, one of Luukkonen or Lyon could go back to Winnipeg to help provide stability in the post-Hellebuyck era. The Sabres also own all their first-round picks for the foreseeable future, and it would probably take at least two of those to pry Hellebuyck from the Jets. Just a year ago, it would be hard to believe that Hellebuyck would waive his no-movement clause to go to Buffalo, but it seems very plausible now.

When the Devils traded Jacob Markstrom to the Florida Panthers in July, I assumed it would only be a matter of time until Hellebuyck was in Newark. To this point, nothing has come to fruition, but New Jersey absolutely needs an NHL-caliber starter. Jake Allen, David Rittich and Nico Daws are on the roster right now, and that won't get the job done.

The Devils were undone by poor goaltending -- and subpar defense -- too much last year. Markstrom allowed 13.7 goals above average, per Natural Stat Trick, and even a diminished version of Hellebuyck would provide a significant upgrade over those numbers. New Jersey has the draft capital to get something done, but it also needs to clear cap space, possibly with a Dougie Hamilton salary dump.

They just won the Stanley Cup with Brandon Bussi in net, so the Hurricanes have proven they don't need elite goaltending to go all the way. Regardless, running it back with Bussi wouldn't be a very safe bet, and adding Hellebuyck would only reinforce Carolina's biggest strength. As we saw in the playoffs, the Hurricanes are already one of the best defensive teams in the league. Put Hellebuyck behind that lineup, and scoring on Carolina becomes a Herculean task.

Thanks to savvy cap management by GM Eric Tulsky, Carolina has the room to add Hellebuyck without jettisoning a current player. Oh, and Carolina has three first-round picks over the next two years. If Tulsky wants to pursue Hellebuyck, he has the resources to do it.

This is more of a longshot, but it would be fun to see the Sharks take a swing if this, assuming Hellebuyck would sign off on a trade to San Jose. Despite the fact that they overpaid for it, the Sharks blue line is better, and there is a wealth of young talent at forward. Another factor here is the current state of the Pacific Division, which is abhorrent. The third playoff spot is up for grabs, and the Sharks could secure it with Hellebuyck in goal.

Superstar Macklin Celebrini is on the final year of his entry-level deal before his $18.8 million salary kicks in next year, so San Jose needs to push the chips in here and figure out the finances next summer. Would the team be willing to trade blue-chip goaltending prospect Yaroslav Askarov to the Jets? San Jose may also need to sweeten a trade package -- or involve a third team -- in order to get Winnipeg to take on Logan Couture's contract.

Yes, Vegas is currently $8.6 million over the salary cap, so there would need to be some impressive maneuvering in order to acquire Hellebuyck. But the Golden Knights have already proven to be cutthroat if a player they covet hits the trade market. Besides, goaltending was optional in Sin City last season. Four goalies combined to allow 23.2 goals above average. Only five teams had a worse save percentage than the Golden Knights.

So, how could the Golden Knights pull off a Hellebuyck blockbuster? Trading an injured Alex Pietrangelo and his contract would help, but that may be costly. Veteran defenseman Brayden McNabb only has a modified no-trade clause, and the same is true for forwards Tomas Hertl, William Karlsson and Ivan Barbashev. Like I said, this would be tough, but the Golden Knights are one team willing to drop the entire egg carton on the floor to make a few omelettes.

#2723. 2026 MLB playoff bracket: Schedule and scores as Milwaukee Brewers win first World Series title

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The Milwaukee Brewers are the 2026 World Series champions.

With Wednesday's Game 5 win – and series win over the Tampa Bay Rays – the Brewers have secured the first title in franchise history on the heels of a dominant postseason. This win ends one of the longest title droughts in MLB history; at 57 years, Milwaukee was behind only the Cleveland Guardians (78 seasons) and San Diego Padres (58 seasons).

The Rays, meanwhile, go back to the drawing board after their elimination as they continue the search for their first World Series title.

Here's the full 2026 MLB playoff bracket and every result from the postseason.

#2722. Did the Chiefs have the most crushing loss of Week 8?

Disappointing. Discouraging. Disheartening.

Many adjectives could describe the feelings after the (5-2) Kansas City Chiefs' fourth-quarter collapse against the (6-2) Denver Broncos in Week 8, but Chiefs Kingdom isn't alone in suffering a crushing defeat this weekend.

Before the Chiefs kicked off later in the afternoon, the Dallas Cowboys blew a 24-10 lead in the fourth quarter against the Arizona Cardinals in front of the home fans at AT&T Stadium. Former Chiefs backup quarterback Gardner Minshew turned back the clock and delivered a vintage gunslinger performance to lead Arizona back and win 33-30 in overtime.

It kept Dallas from improving to 4-4 and closing in on the (4-2-1) New York Giants in a division that feels completely up for grabs, with the shortcomings of the (2-4-1) Washington Commanders and (2-6) Philadelphia Eagles this season.

According to the national fans in SB Nation's 'Reacts' poll from Sunday, the Cowboys' collapse was the only loss that was more "crushing" than the Chiefs losing their grip on the game and the division race in Denver.

The (4-4) Baltimore Ravens were in a close third place after a blowout loss to the Buffalo Bills on the road; the crushing part of the defeat was the news that quarterback Lamar Jackson (thumb) may be out for the rest of the season.

Do you think the Chiefs were the most crushing defeat of Week 8? Or does Dallas take the cake? Let us know in the comments.

#2720. Avalanche sign star Cale Makar to largest contract in NHL history at $163.2 million

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

This has been the summer of spending in the NHL, and the Colorado Avalanche just broke the bank for star defenseman Cale Makar. On Friday, the team announced that Makar signed an eight-year extension worth $163.2 million.

The contract is the richest in league history -- both in total value and annual value at $20.4 million per year. Makar is the first player in NHL history to cross the $20 million salary threshold.

At 27-years old, Makar is arguably the best defensemen in the NHL, and he will remain in that conversation for quite a while. Makar has already won the Norris Trophy twice, and he was a key factor in Colorado's Stanley Cup run, which ended with him winning the Conn Smythe Trophy.

This past season, Makar posted monster numbers from the blue line yet again as he tallied 20 goals and 59 assists in 75 games. Since entering the NHL in 2019, Makar leads all defensemen in goals (20) and points (507).

"I am so excited and humbled to continue to wear the Avalanche sweater and represent this incredible organization and community," Makar said in a statement. "I would like to thank the Kroenke family, Joe Sakic and the entire Avalanche organization for their commitment, trust and belief in me. I would also like to thank my wife and family for their constant support, as well as Scott, Brian and Steve Bartlett for their guidance and assistance.

"To the Avalanche fans, thank you for your continued support and for welcoming us to Colorado years ago. Colorado has truly become a second home to us, and we are so grateful to be here. I look forward to getting back on the ice with my teammates and continuing to work toward our goal of bringing the Stanley Cup back to the Mile High City."

Over the last three seasons, Makar's impact has been immense in all three zones. He's posted a five-on-five expected goals share of 54.5%, per Natural Stat Trick. With Makar on the ice in those situations, Colorado has outscored its opponents 220-157. In two of the last three seasons, Makar has hit the 90-point mark, and he has 100-point potential at his best.

Makar's $11.4 million raise kicks in in at the start of the 2026-27 season, and it will make things significantly tighter for the Avalanche as they navigate the salary cap. The bright side for Colorado -- and the key in getting this deal done -- is that the team now has each of its core players signed through at least 2031.

There may be some sticker shock upon seeing the $20.4 million average annual value, but that's the new reality in the NHL as the cap rises exponentially. The previous record for highest salary in NHL history was just set earlier this offseason when the Anaheim Ducks signed center Leo Carlsson to a contract worth $18 million per year, and the Chicago Blackhawks announced a $75 million extension for Connor Bedard.

The next domino to fall will be an extension for Minnesota Wild defenseman Quinn Hughes, who is neck-and-neck with Makar as the best defensemen in the world. Waiting until after the Makar deal was announced to sign Hughes could prove to be a mistake for Wild general manager Bill Guerin.

#2719. The 2026 Brewers were the team of destiny all along -- and now they're World Series champions

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

ST. PETERSBURG, Fla. -- Since the 2026 playoffs began, we've been using words like "relentless" and "inevitable" when it comes to the Milwaukee Brewers. As they finished their coronation in Game 5 with a 4-1 victory over the Rays to take the series and win their first title in franchise history, it's pretty clear that the Brewers were the team of destiny from early in the season.

Game 5 on Wednesday never really felt like the Rays' night. Their pitching situation was dire after needing to use Nick Martinez in Game 4. The offense was dealing with Jacob Misiorowski and a full Brewers bullpen of studs. Junior Caminero was compromised and Yandy Díaz would join him. It just never felt like it was going to be their night and it wasn't, because of course it wasn't. It was the Brewers' night. The Brewers' series. The Brewers' season.

It took some longer than others to see it, myself admittedly included. They struggled in two different spans in April, with a six-game losing streak and later a four-game losing streak. That was about it, though. After a loss on May 4 dropped them to 18-16, the Brewers went absolutely bonkers the rest of the season: 85-43. And now they've completed an 11-2 playoff run against stacked competition. That means they finished 96-45. Holy smokes.

It's a misnomer to act like this is some ragtag bunch; a motley crew, island of the misfit toys collection of big-league players. The Brewers have stars. They have a former MVP in Christian Yelich and, though he's past his prime, he was a quality contributor in the playoffs. Jacob Misiorowski is going to win the National League Cy Young. Jackson Chourio is an emerging superstar. William Contreras is an All-Star catcher. Brice Turang is arguably the best second baseman in baseball.

The Brewers are such a well-rounded bunch and the front office thrives on winning on the margins, which is why some might slap inappropriate labels on them, such as the little engine that could. That doesn't begin to cover it. They are, in fact, a team of destiny getting contributions from all over the place.

There are more examples, of course. It was just a team full of winners. No matter what obstacle popped up for the Brewers this season, and especially in the playoffs, they reacted by winning. For crying out loud, they scored eight runs with two outs in the World Series. That was off-the-charts outrageous in Game 3.

Then there were the injuries. Yelich and Chourio missed time. Quinn Priester was lost for the season in spring training. Brandon Woodruff missed most of the year before making the World Series roster. With these injuries, in addition to the trade of Peralta, the door was open for players like Drohan, Gasser and Logan Henderson to make the most of their opportunities.

That's just what happens with teams of destiny. It's that ol' "next man up" mentality. The Brewers have been practicing this for years, trading away established veterans before they reach free agency and immediately replacing them with inexperienced players or even retreads like Jake Bauers who are ready to step in. For every player out the door, there's someone else ready to shine.

The Brewers just won 11 playoff games and came from behind to win six times. That's beyond ridiculous. Overall, teams scoring first in the playoffs were 27-13. Milwaukee accounted for six of the losses in there, bludgeoning its opponents even after falling behind.

The "destiny" thumbprints were all over this series. Even in their Game 4 loss, they just kept coming. And they responded to that loss by getting ace-level work from Misiorowski in Game 5 and putting up run-scoring rallies in three of the first six innings. They didn't need any home runs. They strung together winning plays on singles and doubles and sacrifice flies. They can win on home runs, too, as they've done a few times this postseason. They just find ways to win, almost every time. Their starters, bullpen, defense and clutch hitting are just too good. That's what it takes to win, and the Brewers won it all.

I mentioned the early struggles at the start of the season. They closed in opposite fashion. Including the playoffs, the Brewers were 26-5 after Sept. 6. They had established themselves as the best team in baseball from May 4 until early September, and then went into overdrive. How is it even possible for a 103-win team to level up and get better?

Relentless. Inevitable. The team of destiny. Milwaukee Brewers, World Series champions.

#2718. 2026 NHL free agent tracker: Patrick Kane returns to Blackhawks on two-year contract

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

NHL free agency has slowed down in late July, but some notable names are still coming off the board. Most notably, Patrick Kane is returning to the Chicago Blackhawks on a two-year contract worth $16 million, the team announced on Thursday.

Already a Chicago legend and future Hall of Famer, Kane won three Stanley Cups and a slew of individual awards with the franchise that selected him with the No. 1 overall pick in 2007. Kane's first stint with the Blackhawks lasted 16 seasons, and he piled up 446 goals and 779 assists in 1,161 games. Kane ranks inside the top five of just about every statistical category in franchise history, and he will get the chance to keep climbing those lists over the next two years.

The Blackhawks sent Kane to the New York Rangers at the 2023 NHL trade deadline, and he played just 19 regular season games there before hitting free agency the next summer. Hip surgery delayed Kane's decision until November of that year, but he ultimately landed with the Detroit Red Wings, where he's spent the last three seasons.

Last season was Kane's best with Detroit, and it proved that he still had some gas left in the tank, at least offensively. Kane posted 16 goals and 41 assists in 67 games -- with 19 of those points coming on the power play.

In his second stint with the Blackhawks, Kane will add another level of playmaking to the power play, and he should strengthen a top six forward group that's bereft of proven talent. Kane will also be an excellent mentor and sounding board for fellow No. 1 overall pick, Connor Bedard, who is set to miss the first month of the season after undergoing shoulder surgery.

Kane isn't the player he once was, but he'll give Chicago a bit more pop at the top of the lineup, and his first game back in a Blackhawks jersey will be a huge night for the city.

#2716. Somerset girls soccer earns District 5-8 Class 2A title berth

SOMERSET — Senior captain Lily Witt delivered four goals, guiding Somerset past Obama Academy 6-0 in a District 5-8 Class 2A girls soccer semifinal, Monday, Nov. 2.

"This feels good," said Witt of the win. "I am excited. All of us seniors have been playing together for so long and it feels really good to make it this far because we haven't in our four seasons."

Somerset will play in its first district title match since 2018 and is seeking its first championship since 2013.

Playoff outlook 4 Somerset County girls soccer teams enter D5 playoffs chasing gold

Breakout players of 2026 Boys, girls soccer players having breakout years in Somerset County

Neither team generated much offense until the 10', when Witt rang the crossbar.

Third-seeded Obama Academy had its lone scoring chance of the first half, when Zenia Cook found some space, but Somerset senior keeper Raisa Seslow came out of the net and stopped a low shot.

The second-seeded Golden Eagles finally broke through in the 14'. Riley DeWitt made a great feed to Anna Moon, who was hauled down by Obama Academy sophomore keeper Janie Allan, leading to a penalty kick. Witt buried the PK for a 1-0 advantage.

Allan kept Obama Academy in the contest early with a save on Moon 2 minutes later. She stoned Witt and Moon again in the 24' and 26', respectively.

However, Somerset continued to play as the aggressor. Witt battled for a ball in the box and beat Allan for a 2-0 lead in the 28'.

DeWitt recorded another quality scoring chance before the break but was denied by Allan.

Somerset added to its lead less than 8 minutes into the second half. Moon broke free, found space and delivered a low, driving shot into the back of the net for a 3-0 advantage.

Witt notched the hat trick in the 10' of the second half, heading in a free kick from Moon for a 4-0 lead. Witt tacked on one more goal less than 6 minutes later as the Golden Eagles held a commanding 5-0 advantage.

Sophie Spangler added a goal late in contest, setting the final.

"Our game plan was just to play one game at a time," Somerset coach Renee Ross said. "We wanted to try and get through this one and get to the title game. The girls stepped up as a team and played with a lot of heart. They wanted to move on to the next round, and we are excited for a rematch with Bedford."

Somerset meets defending champion, Bedford in the championship match, Wednesday, Nov. 4. Bedford defeated Somerset 6-1, Monday, Oct. 12, in its regular season matchup. The Bisons have qualified for an impressive 10 straight PIAA playoffs.

"We are going to work on our formations just to be able to keep up with them," Witt said of the matchup with Bedford. "They're good at passing, so we want to be able to cut off their triangles, communicate and work well with each other."

#2713. 2026-27 NHL season predictions: Expert picks for Stanley Cup playoffs, champion and major awards this season

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Following a brief offseason and an expedited preseason, the 2026-27 NHL season has arrived. Before the puck drops on opening night, we have some predictions to make.

If this season feel like it's staring earlier than usual, that's because it is. The NHL is adopting an 84-game regular season, which just means more hockey for the rest of us. That also means a long and arduous journey to the Stanley Cup is ever so slightly more difficult.

So, what will that journey look like? The Carolina Hurricanes are looking to defend their title as Cup champions, but they'll have to contend with a deep Eastern Conference and some real juggernauts in the Western Conference. With that in mind, we logged our expert predictions for the playoff field in each conference -- and there were a few surprises -- before making our Stanley Cup Final selections.

We also called our shot on the winners of each major individual award. Connor McDavid is looking for his fourth Hart Trophy, but he's got some competition from an up-and-coming superstar in his own division. Races for the Norris Trophy and Vezina Trophy should be pretty wide open, and unexpected candidates always emerge throughout the season.

It should be a fun season in the NHL, so follow along with all our coverage at CBS Sports. Let's get things kicked off with the season preview and predictions, which will of course be 100% accurate.

Baumgartner: I don't see many new faces entering the conversation when it comes to the Eastern Conference playoff race. #2712. Kovac feels Dortmund "earned a deserved point" against Bayern

Borussia Dortmund found themselves a goal down on two occasions at the Allianz Arena on Saturday night, but came back both times to ultimately earn a 2-2 draw against Bayern Munich.

Speaking in a post-match interview, Dortmund coach Niko Kovac praised his team, arguing they deserved the point despite being clearly second-best in terms of shots and chances created.

“If you have 50 percent possession here in Munich against Bayern, then you've done a lot of things right. We didn't allow them very much," Kovac said.

"Naturally, we came under a little more pressure in the second half, but the team continued to believe in themselves even after falling behind. We played with a back four, tried to press higher up the pitch and match Bayern's runs, particularly those of their full-backs, who repeatedly move inside. The lads did that brilliantly.

"Coming back from behind twice here in Munich is no easy task. That's why I'm convinced we earned a deserved point today.”

#2710. Lions signing veteran LB off Patriots practice squad

According to a report from ESPN's Adam Schefter, the Detroit Lions are signing veteran linebacker K.J. Britt off of the New England Patriots' practice squad.

The move comes after Detroit suffered two linebacker injuries on Sunday against the Minnesota Vikings. Rookie Jimmy Rolder suffered a season-ending hamstring injury, while Jack Campbell remains in the hospital with a neck injury. No specifics are known at this point about Campbell's situation, but the team did say that early developments were "positive."

Britt was a fifth-round draft pick for the Tampa Bay Buccaneers in 2021, and ended up playing 59 games for them over four seasons—including 15 starts. Last year, he was with the Miami Dolphins, playing in all 17 games and starting one. This year, he failed to make the Patriots' 53-man roster, but they kept him around on the practice squad, and he was elevated for the team's Week 5 game against the Raiders.

For most of his career, Britt has served as a special teamer. Across just over five seasons in the NFL, Britt has played 1,303 special teams snaps vs. 994 on defense—and he has logged over 250 special teams snaps in four of his first five NFL seasons.

There's a good chance that will be Britt's role early in Detroit. With the Lions likely to lean more heavily on the likes of Malcolm Rodriguez, Derrick Barnes, and Trevor Nowaske on defense—with potentially Amen Ogbongbemiga and Joe Bachie mixed in—the Lions will need more experienced depth on special teams and reserves on defense.

Britt should be a relatively good fit in Detroit, as a high-effort player who was a captain at Auburn.

Detroit should have a good handle on who Britt is as a person and player, as he overlapped with current Lions defensive line coach Kacy Rodgers during his entire time with the Buccaneers.

#2709. What's next for the Rays? Three questions after World Series loss, including payroll and Junior Caminero

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The Tampa Bay Rays' 2026 season is over. Coming off a 98-win regular season, the American League East title, and their third-ever pennant, the Rays were eliminated in five games from the 2026 World Series by the juggernaut Milwaukee Brewers. There's pride to be taken in the Rays' overall body of work this year, but the prevailing reality is that they failed to achieve their ultimate goal. While the Brewers are celebrating their first World Series title, the Rays remain on the list of franchises that have never hoisted the trophy.

This all means that the Rays now turn their attention to 2027 and whether they're likely to make another run at relevance, contention, and perhaps another bite at the championship apple. Let's explore that now by exploring three questions that will define the Rays' coming offseason and their 2027 campaign.

The Rays, across so much of their history, have reliably punched above their weight. The foundation of that has been a level of support and investment from club ownership that's been entirely lacking. The present ownership group, led by Jacksonville real estate something or other Patrick Zalupski, took over in September of 2025, so this is almost wholly an indictment of former owner Stuart Sternberg.

Since the Rays' current run of contention began with their 90-win campaign in 2018, the club has never ranked higher than 24th in Opening Day active roster payroll and never higher than 22nd in luxury tax payroll (basically 40-man roster payroll based on average annual value of contracts). Twice in that span, the Rays have ranked last in year-end 40-man payroll. Given that the current revenue-sharing structure gives small-market teams more than enough to cover cash payrolls, this is all a plain dereliction of duties on the part of Sternberg. One of the canniest front offices in all of sports and the rosters they have assembled deserve more buy-in from ownership.

Maybe now that Zalupski's group is in charge and the Rays finally appear to have a new ballpark – heavily funded by tax dollars – across the finish line, things will change on this front. It'd be a fine time for that, given that the Rays right now are self-evidently capable of vying for a title. This winter, they have a number of key contributors due for arbitration raises, including Shane McClanahan, Jonathan Aranda, Griffin Jax, and Bryan Baker. Yandy Díaz in August triggered a $13 million vesting option for 2027, which, based on prior organizational behavior, might make him a trade candidate. There are also a number of investments needed in the rotation, which we'll get into below.

There's also the matter of what the next collective bargaining agreement will look like once one is agreed to and the forthcoming lockout ends. Signs point to the next CBA being of benefit to teams like the Rays, and that may inform some of their offseason behavior before that owner-implemented lockout likely begins on the night of Dec. 1.

The need for better stewardship from owners will be acutely critical to the makeup of the 2027 rotation. McClanahan's incoming arb raise – which will likely give him the highest salary of any Rays arb-eligible player this offseason — makes him a trade candidate. Freddy Peralta, their stabilizer after he was acquired leading up to the trade deadline, is a free agent. Drew Rasmussen has an $8 million option/$500,000 buyout for 2027. It should be a no-brainer to exercise said option, but, well, you know. There's also Nick Martinez, who has a $20 million mutual option for next season. That likely means he's headed for free agency, but the Rays should still pick up their end of it on principle. That's 80% of the rotation, at least as it was constituted after the trade deadline and before McClanahan's forearm injury. In all, that quartet of starters combined for a 3.03 ERA during the regular season and 42 of the team's 54 quality starts. They also combined for a WAR of 11.2, and that's despite getting just 10 starts from Peralta.

The Rays will need to do a lot of retention work on this front if they're going to again be the best team in the American League next season. As it stands right now, the rotation is the biggest unknown going into the offseason.

The 23-year-old third baseman and emerging superstar is the lineup fulcrum for Tampa Bay. Since his rookie season of 2024, Caminero has improved his OPS+ from 105 to 130 to 144 this past season. His WAR has swollen from 0.7 in 43 games as a rookie to 4.3 in 2025 to 5.7 in 2026.

He hits the ball exceptionally hard, and he has the fastest bat speed in the game. Additionally, Caminero in 2026 made impressive strides in terms of his plate discipline. Prospects these days, thanks to developmental and training advances at the lower levels, are arriving more ready than ever, and some start off at their peak. Caminero, though, has shown improvement on a number of forward-looking fronts, and that raises the compelling possibility that he still has some growth in him. Is a 50-homer season and an MVP award in the offing? That seems quite possible, and given the unbalanced nature of the Rays' lineup, they could use even more from their best player.

#2708. Red Sox's Japanese Offseason Target Predicted to Land $180M Contract

Red Sox's Japanese Offseason Target Predicted to Land $180M Contract originally appeared on NESN. Add NESN as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Slugging Japanese superstar Teruaki Sato has reportedly been posted, giving teams like the Boston Red Sox and others around Major League Baseball an opportunity to sign him in this 45-day window.

There's a hope and expectation that Sato is going to be one of the better hitters in Major League Baseball when he comes over from Japan, though as the Red Sox and other teams have found out, there's never a guarantee that guys are going to translate into the players that everybody hopes for them to be.

In regard to what Sato might be looking for in a potential contract, he's not one of the guys who can only be had for international money. The Red Sox and other teams in baseball are going to have to pay him a pretty penny.

I think he stays at third base initially and would be a perfect fit for teams like the Phillies, Red Sox, Angels and Yankees, which leads me to believe this will end up being a big-market bidding war," Jim Bowden wrote.

Sato is coming off a ridiculous season in the NPB for Hanshin, posting a .991 OPS, a .306 batting average, and hitting 39 home runs with 106 RBIs.

There's a strong argument to be made that he's the best offensive player available in baseball this offseason, and to see that the Red Sox might be interested is a very promising sign.

#2706. Brewers win the 2026 World Series: Where the Brew Crew ranks among the best recent MLB champions

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

ST. PETERSBURG -- The Milwaukee Brewers are World Series champions. There's so much weight in such a statement on its own, but let's consider for a second all the context behind it.

The 2026 Brewers won 103 games in the regular season and then, more impressively, went a ridiculous 11-2 in the playoffs, never facing elimination, never even trailing in a series. They were one loss away Not only that, but they came so close to being the first team ever to go 8-0 in the League Championship Series and World Series. The 1976 Reds were the only team to sweep both the LCS and World Series, but that only got them to 7-0 because the LCS was five games until 1985. The best playoff record in the era of multiple wild cards was the 2022 Astros at 11-2, and the Brewers have tied that. The 2005 White Sox went 11-1 and so did the 1999 Yankees. We're already up in rarefied air. But there's more.

Think about how hot the Brewers closed. They finished the regular season by winning 15 of their last 18 games. This means they went 26-5 after Sept. 6. Further, look at who they decimated in the playoffs. The Padres were riding a run of 19 wins in 22 games, including a two-game sweep in the Wild Card Series. The Brewers went 3-1 in that NLDS. The Dodgers were the two-time defending champions who swept the Brewers in the NLCS last year. They won 100 games in 2026. The Brewers swept them in four games. The Rays won 98 games in the regular season and were 7-2 thus far in the playoffs. The Brewers took them down in five with their only loss coming in 12 innings.

Remember the Bob Uecker line from "Major League?" There's nothing left but a vapor trail.

That's the Brewers' path to the championship. They just became an absolute killing machine down the stretch, pulverizing everything in their path.

Statistically, they can't touch the 1998 Yankees (114 wins, +309 run differential, 11-2 playoff record including World Series sweep) and certainly can't get to the 1927 Yankees (110-44, +376 run differential, swept World Series). But what about more recently?

Such an exercise is subjective, but let's figure out where these Brewers rank in recent memory. Probably really close to the top, if not all the way up. This wasn't a team loaded with future Hall of Famers, as the Dodgers were last season, for example, but the numbers say these Brewers take a back seat to very few, if any, teams in the past couple of decades.

In fact, let's sort through it. I'll go with the multiple-wild card era, so we're starting in 2012. Let's narrow it down to teams that won at least 100 games and also won the World Series. Yes, the World Series title is a requirement, so we can safely say goodbye to the 2021 Giants and 2022 Dodgers.

We'll compare the teams via regular-season record, run differential, playoff record and how they fared in the World Series. As you'll see, the Brewers will have the most impressive resume here.

An absolute juggernaut in the regular season outside of a wretched stretch before the All-Star break, the Cubs finally broke the faux curse and won it all. They had the regular-season MVP and two Cy Young candidates, in addition to the Cy Young winner from the previous year. On the downside of this argument, they trailed in both the NLCS and World Series (three games to one). Coming back is impressive, but the most dominant teams didn't fall behind.

The first World Series title in franchise history came with a young and (at least it felt like it at the time) fun team. They had the MVP and a former Cy Young winner and also traded for future Hall of Famer Justin Verlander. They needed seven games in both the ALCS and World Series.

The highest win total on the list is right here. They also never faced elimination in the playoffs, though they did trail 1-0 in the ALCS. The MVP of the league was again on the champs and they also had three Cy Young winners (Chris Sale hadn't won his yet, but he was the undisputed ace).

After a 106-win regular season, the Astros steamrolled through the American League side of the bracket, going 7-0. They did lose Games 1 and 3, though, to fall behind 2-1 in the World Series after seeing the Phillies club them for a bunch of home runs in a 7-0 Game 3 win. The Astros righted the ship thereafter, though, and won the next three games, including two nail-biters.

Though the regular seasons are pretty comparable in that chart above, the Brewers were easily the top dog in the postseason. They lost one game between the NLCS and World Series.

We should probably loop in the 2013 Red Sox, the 2015 Royals and the repeat champion 2024-25 Dodgers as well.

If I had to rank them, I think the Mount Rushmore in this era would be:

The 2025 Dodgers were a tough omission, but they didn't even earn a bye due to a relatively low regular-season win total.

I'm willing to hear arguments for the Brewers over that Red Sox team, too. After all, the Red Sox did lose a whole three playoff games, even though they never faced elimination.

The bottom line is that the 2026 Milwaukee Brewers are one of the best, if not the greatest, champions in recent history.
